  running a script, or many users and processes at once. Judge scale against
  that, and say which load you assumed.

## 2. Look for

1. **Bug:** wrong result, crash, missed edge case (empty, zero, last item,
   rounding, time zones), a caller broken by the change, a fix applied in one
   caller while the shared function stays broken.
2. **Risk:** security holes (injection, weak randomness, secrets, missing
   checks on input from users), data loss (errors swallowed, writes in the
   wrong order, no transaction).
3. **Scale:** fine for one user, wrong for many: check-then-write races, the
   same work done by every process, memory or lists that only grow, a query
   per item, O(n^2) on big input, per-process state that must be shared.
4. **Missing test:** risky new logic (a branch, a parser, money, security,
   data writes, a bug fix) with no test that fails when it breaks. One good
   test, not coverage.
5. **Speed:** big slowdowns are problems. Small wins (work repeated in a hot
   loop) are suggestions; some software counts every millisecond.
6. **Lean:** code that should not exist or should be smaller.
   - delete: dead code, unused options, speculative features
   - reuse: the repo already has this helper (name the path)
   - stdlib / native: the standard library or platform already does it; a
     new dependency for a few lines
   - yagni: abstraction with one implementation, config nobody sets
   - merge: near-copies that must change together
   - split: one function doing several unrelated jobs, so it is hard to read
     or test. Split by job, never by line count, and never into helpers that
     exist only to make a function shorter.

## 3. Check before you report

- Every finding needs a concrete case: "this input or situation leads to this
  wrong result". No case, no finding.
- Re-read the lines and confirm: the caller exists, the value can really be
  empty, the code really is unused.
- A shortcut marked with a `shortcut:` (or older `ponytail:`) comment that names its limit is a
  decision, not a finding, unless the expected load already crosses it.
- Propose the smallest fix that works. Prefer fixes that delete code. Never
  add layers, frameworks or config the problem does not need.
- No style taste, no "consider", no vague worries.

## 4. Output

Very simple English: short sentences, everyday words. Explain a technical
term the first time you use it. The reader may never have seen this code.

Start with `What this change does:` in two or three sentences.

Then the findings in three groups, skip empty groups:
- **Must fix:** bug, security, data loss, breaks at the expected load.
- **Should fix:** risky code without a test, real slowness, duplication, a
  function that mixes jobs, code that should not exist.
- **Nice to have:** small speed-ups, shorter forms.

Number findings across all groups, so the user can say "fix 2 and 5".
Every finding has all four parts, each one or two short sentences:

2. **Orders land on the wrong day** (`billing/close_day.py:L40-52`)
   - **What this is:** At midnight this job closes the day and bills all orders of that day.
   - **Problem:** It takes "today" from the server clock, which runs in UTC. An order placed
     at 00:30 in Berlin is billed on the day before.
   - **Fix:** Compute the day once in the shop's time zone:
     `datetime.now(ZoneInfo("Europe/Berlin")).date()`. One line, nothing else changes.
   - **If we skip it:** Late orders show the wrong date, and accounting fixes them by hand.

End with:
- `Verdict: Ship.` or `Verdict: fix 1 and 3 first.`
- `Lean: -<N> lines possible.` when lean findings exist.
- `Not checked:` one line, if something mattered and you could not check it.

Nothing found: `What this change does:`, then `Looks good. Ship.` and one line
on what you checked.

Lists findings, changes no code.

kind: code

  - scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh

threat_table:
  - control: scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh
    disposition: "residual: the fixed Python helper reads records as data, writes only the replan file and the conditional decisions row, and never executes record text or dispatches work; the temporary-directory tests verify these effects."
  - control: tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py
    disposition: "residual: script executions use a copied script and fixture records in temporary directories; the vocabulary test reads the real learning tree without changing it."
  - control: .orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/**
    disposition: "residual: branch artifacts stay in this task's directory and contain the dispatched copy, source attribution and validation evidence for the final-head audit."
  - control: the task-file copy on the branch (.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/task.md)
    disposition: "residual: the copy is byte-identical to the dispatched text; the TASK and RESULT bind its own sha256 and the orchestrator compares it at acceptance."
  - control: the branch evidence records under .orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/ (report.md, validation.md, sandbox.md, learning.md and worker review evidence)
    disposition: "residual: records contain prose and command output; sandbox.md lists every refused command and the audit compares the claims with the final diff and CI."
  - control: task metadata
    disposition: "residual: the orchestrator checks C1 to C10 and C7b before dispatch and at acceptance; the byte comparison authenticates the complete dispatched front matter."
  - control: metadata separators
    disposition: "residual: the helper reads only the bounded first YAML front-matter document of the input task; the task-copy byte comparison covers every separator."
  - control: in-process effects
    disposition: "residual: the helper creates only .orchestration/tasks/<task>-replan.md and conditionally appends one row to .orchestration/decisions.tsv; it preserves the source task and existing replans and executes no command derived from records."
  - control: workflow and interpreter or environment inputs
    disposition: "residual: no workflow or environment configuration changes; the script runs its embedded Python helper through uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -I and loads YAML with yaml.safe_load."
  - control: new or renamed scripts

  - control: orchestrator effect merge

# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01 — plan 024 P1-13 hold playbook

## Goal

`scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh <task> <class>` expands a pinned corrective procedure into a replan skeleton and records a pending decision when its prospective changes exceed the task's `allowed_files`. It implements item (13) of `.orchestration/plans/024-design-programs.md` and the class-specific playbook of `.orchestration/plans/024-design-quality.md` section 5.13. This task is `security: false`: the script reads records and writes records and executes no PR-controlled input. Existing controls own hold detection at three findings of one class, INV-6's S2 thresholds including two revises, and a P0 finding. The P0 rule remains an immediate revert PR for a defect already on main and hold for an unmerged artifact.

## Files

1. `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` is a new tracked mapping keyed by class slug. It contains one entry for every distinct slug in the `classes:` lines of `.orchestration/learning/mistakes/M-*.md`. The worker recounts the records and distinct slugs at start and records both counts in `validation.md`.

   Each entry has `remedy:` and `blast_radius:`. The remedy is one or two plain sentences describing a corrective procedure derived from the records' Corrections lines and their references in `.orchestration/learning/corrections-inventory.tsv`. The blast radius is a list of repository-relative globs naming files a fix of that class touches, derived from the records' Evidence lines. The report attributes each entry to its source mistake records and correction references. A class without a known corrective procedure has `remedy: unknown` and `blast_radius: []`. Review titles and illustrative class names do not add vocabulary entries.

   `tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py::HoldPlaybookTests::test_vocabulary_covers_mistake_classes` reads the real mistake records and asserts that every recorded slug appears in this mapping. `tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py::HoldPlaybookTests::test_known_class_without_remedy_is_reported` verifies the explicit unknown-remedy representation.

2. `scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh` accepts exactly `<task> <class>`. A small Python helper embedded in the shell script performs parsing and record generation through `uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -I`. It uses `yaml.safe_load` for the class mapping and the task's first YAML front-matter document. Shell comments use English shdoc annotations.

   The task id matches `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$`. An invalid task id exits 2 before constructing output paths. A class absent from `classes.yaml` exits 2 and names the supplied slug in its diagnostic. These refusals leave the replan and decision ledger unchanged.

   The helper reads `.orchestration/tasks/<task>.md` for `allowed_files` and creates `.orchestration/tasks/<task>-replan.md`. Creation refuses an existing filesystem entry at that destination with exit 4 and preserves its contents. The script never edits the source task text and never dispatches.

   The replan contains the cause class, the remedy sentence, every blast-radius glob, and the task's allowed-files entries that cover each glob. Uncovered globs are explicitly marked outside `allowed_files`. Coverage concerns the entire prospective scope denoted by a glob, including paths that do not exist yet. Matching a pattern string as though it were a filename or expanding only currently existing files does not establish coverage.

   The replan includes a new Completion sentence skeleton with placeholders for the observable result, verification selectors and evidence. It includes this four-field line:

   ```text
   re-plan: specification — <fill>; design — <fill>; implementation — <fill>; verification — <fill>
   ```

   The four fields follow the structure of the S1 re-plan in `.orchestration/acceptance/dotfiles-T133-permission-guard-gate-a04.md` and its `replans` JSON object. The skeleton remains a proposal until the orchestrator supplies concrete content. The script leaves the acceptance record unchanged.

   Every blast-radius glob covered by `allowed_files` produces exit 0. The orchestrator fills the skeleton and re-dispatches through the applicable hold procedure. A known class with `remedy: unknown` is reported explicitly with its empty blast radius; it follows the same coverage rule and acquires no invented corrective procedure. Exit 0 establishes scope coverage only.

   Any uncovered glob produces the replan, appends exactly one six-column TSV row to `.orchestration/decisions.tsv`, and exits 3. The row has `id` equal to `D-<task>-replan`, `when` equal to the current UTC timestamp, `what` naming the uncovered globs, `why` naming the class, `basis` equal to `hold-playbook`, and `result` equal to `pending`. It contains no default or deadline that consumes a default.

   `tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py::HoldPlaybookTests::test_three_classes_expand_into_replans`, `test_uncovered_scope_appends_pending_decision`, `test_unknown_class_exits_2`, `test_existing_replan_exits_4`, `test_invalid_task_id_is_refused`, `test_scope_comparison_covers_the_whole_glob` and `test_record_text_is_never_executed` verify these statements.

3. `tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py` uses `unittest` and temporary repository layouts containing a copied script, fixture `classes.yaml`, source task text and decision ledger. Its three-class expansion fixture uses `colored-json-parse`, `concurrent-ff-clobber` and `records-form`. Each class produces its own replan skeleton with the expected remedy, scope classification, Completion placeholder and four re-plan fields.

   The scope decision test verifies exit 3, the exact decision id and six fields, preservation of existing ledger rows and absence of a default. Refusal tests verify their exit codes and unchanged records. The overwrite test also repeats an out-of-scope invocation and verifies that exit 4 appends no second decision row. The literal-data test uses command-looking record text and verifies that it remains text without executing.

   The known-class unknown-remedy test verifies the reported `unknown`, empty blast radius and coverage exit status. The whole-glob test includes an exact permitted scope, a scope covered by an allowed directory glob and a broader scope that extends outside the allowed files. The vocabulary test reads the real tree without invoking the script there.

   The behavioral tests are written before implementation and have red results followed by green results. The vocabulary test has a red result before the tracked mapping is added and a green result with the completed mapping.

4. `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/` contains the byte-identical dispatched `task.md`, `report.md`, `validation.md`, `sandbox.md`, `learning.md` and worker review evidence. The report maps the Files requirements to their test selectors and records the class-source attribution. Worker review filenames carry the required `-worker-` infix.

## Out of scope

`dispatch.sh` changes, including refusal of TASK or revise sends during hold, are deferred until P1-6 merges. That follow-on work includes the hold-send-refusal fixture named by the plan. This task has no dependency on that merge.

`verdict.sh`, `scripts/regime-counters.sh`, hold detection, counter output and P0 enforcement remain outside this implementation. The existing S1 and S2 lines supply context for the orchestrator; this helper neither parses review titles into classes nor releases a hold.

Editing any mistake record, correction record, correction inventory or acceptance record is outside this task. Tests exercise replan creation and decision appends in temporary directories. Live recovery records are written only when the orchestrator later invokes the completed helper.

## Check

`make format`, `shellcheck scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh`, `shfmt -d scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh` and `uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -m unittest tests.unit.test_hold_playbook -v` have their commands, verbatim output, exit statuses and tested heads pasted in `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/validation.md`. The script passes shellcheck and shfmt, and the unit module is green.

The three-class expansion tests and the decision, refusal, literal-data and vocabulary tests carry their red-before-implementation and green-final-head evidence. Every script execution uses a temporary fixture tree. No local Bats tests run.

An inherited formatting failure is reproduced on the unchanged base and recorded with its output. It remains a reported failure and receives no repair outside `allowed_files`.

`git diff --numstat origin/main...HEAD` shows at most 350 changed lines outside `tests/` and `.orchestration/`, and at most 400 changed lines under `tests/`. Changed lines are additions plus deletions, and fixtures count against the test cap. Validation includes the totals and changed-file inventory.

The orchestrator runs the format-2 task-consistency checker, including C1 to C10 and C7b, before dispatch and at acceptance. The task copy remains byte-identical to the dispatched file.

## Completion

The Completion clause of `.orchestration/tasks/dotfiles-T133-regime-ci-followup-a01.md` applies with `dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-followup-a01` as the owner of any advisory P2 and `task_copy=<sha256 of .orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/task.md>` in the RESULT. This task's scope, Check, `security: false`, fresh-branch rule and prohibition on `@codex review` comments govern that reference.

One PR starts from a fresh branch off `origin/main`. The orchestrator writes the advisory owner task before recording its first ownership disposition. A small in-scope P2 follows the inherited `check=` revise procedure while INV-6 permits it.

The worker's own pre-push review is one round at most. Every worker-side fix round counts toward INV-6. A surviving P0/P1 is sent in the RESULT as `open:<finding-or-thread-id>` and stops integration for the operator's decision. The third finding of one class stops further rounds until the orchestrator's re-plan.

The RESULT identifies the final head, base, branch, changed files, test selectors, caps, evidence paths, task-copy hash and every Bot finding's disposition. It carries the precheck evidence required by the applicable RESULT procedure.

The orchestrator runs the task-level audit on the final head and dispositions every finding before the gate. Final-head CI, the feedback sweep, audit, acceptance record, gate and merge follow Orchestrator Playbook step 10 in its prescribed order. A changed head receives fresh evidence.

No `@codex review` comment is issued. Every `gh` write and Git write runs as an individual command without shell chaining or a wrapper combining writes. A classifier denial produces a blocked PONG naming the command and refusal.

## Orchestrator note (2026-10-19T03:2xZ): classes.yaml is a record, not a worker file

`.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` is written by the orchestrator (checker C2 keeps every `.orchestration/` path except the task directory out of a worker's allowed_files); the orchestrator seeds it from the `classes:` lines of the mistake records before this task is dispatched and keeps it current. The worker therefore does not edit it: item 1 is read as the file format the script consumes, the pinned-vocabulary check is implemented as `hold-playbook.sh --check-vocabulary <classes.yaml> <mistakes dir>` (exit 2 listing every class slug of the records that the file lacks, exit 0 otherwise) and tested with fixtures only; the orchestrator runs it against the real tree at acceptance and pastes the output. No test reads the real `.orchestration/learning/` tree.

+#!/usr/bin/env bash
+# @file hold-playbook.sh
+# @brief Expand a class's remedy into a replan skeleton and record a pending decision for out-of-scope globs.
+# @description
+#   Plan 024 P1-13 (item (13) of 024-design-programs.md, the class playbook of 024-design-quality.md 5.13). Reads the
+#   class's `remedy:` and `blast_radius:` from `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` and the task's `allowed_files`
+#   from the first YAML front-matter document of `.orchestration/tasks/<task>.md`, both with yaml.safe_load, and
+#   creates `.orchestration/tasks/<task>-replan.md`: the cause class, the remedy, each blast-radius glob with the
+#   allowed_files entries that cover it (or marked outside allowed_files), a Completion sentence and a four-field
+#   `re-plan:` line of `<fill>` placeholders for the orchestrator. The root is the checkout this script lives in.
+#   A glob is covered by an entry when every path the glob can name, existing or not, matches the entry under
+#   regime-ci.py's allowed(): a trailing `/**` takes any path under its literal prefix, and elsewhere `*` and `?`
+#   stay within one path segment. When a glob is uncovered, one six-column row `D-<task>-replan` (basis
+#   `hold-playbook`, result `pending`, no default) is appended to `.orchestration/decisions.tsv`.
+#   Record text is only read and written as data; the script never edits the task text and never dispatches.
+#   With `--check-vocabulary`, it lists every class slug in the `classes:` front-matter lists of `M-*.md` in the
+#   mistakes directory that the class file lacks.
+# @arg $1 string Task id, matching `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$`.
+# @arg $2 string Class slug, a key of classes.yaml's `classes` mapping.
+# @option --check-vocabulary <classes.yaml> <mistakes-dir> Check the class file against the mistake records instead.
+# @exitcode 0 The replan is written and every glob is inside allowed_files; or the vocabulary covers the records.
+# @exitcode 2 Usage error, an invalid task id, a class not in classes.yaml, malformed or missing records; nothing is
+#   written. With --check-vocabulary, also a class slug of the records that the class file lacks.
+# @exitcode 3 The replan is written and the pending decision row is appended: a glob is outside allowed_files.
+# @exitcode 4 The replan destination already exists (a file, a directory or a link); nothing is written.
+# @example
+#   scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01 records-form
+#   scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh --check-vocabulary .orchestration/learning/classes.yaml \
+#       .orchestration/learning/mistakes
+set -euo pipefail
+
+usage() {
+    echo "usage: hold-playbook.sh <task> <class> | hold-playbook.sh --check-vocabulary <classes.yaml> <mistakes-dir>" >&2
+    exit 2
+}
+if (($# == 3)) && [[ $1 == --check-vocabulary ]]; then
+    set -- vocabulary "$2" "$3"
+elif (($# == 2)); then
+    [[ $1 =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$ ]] || {
+        echo "hold-playbook: invalid task id; refused" >&2
+        exit 2
+    }
+    set -- plan "$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)" "$1" "$2"
+else
+    usage
+fi
+exec uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -I - "$@" << 'PY'
+import csv
+import datetime
+import functools
+import os
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import yaml
+
+STAR, QMARK, ANY = object(), object(), object()
+
+
+def fail(message, code=2):
+    print(f"hold-playbook: {message}; refused", file=sys.stderr)
+    sys.exit(code)
+
+
+def load(path):
+    try:
+        return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as error:
+        fail(f"cannot read {path}: {error.__class__.__name__}")
+
+
+def front(path):
+    """The first YAML front-matter document: the lines between the file's first `---` line and the next."""
+    lines = load(path).split("\n")
+    if lines[0] != "---" or "---" not in lines[1:]:
+        fail(f"{path} has no front matter")
+    try:
+        return yaml.safe_load("\n".join(lines[1 : lines.index("---", 1)]))
+    except yaml.YAMLError:
+        fail(f"{path}: the front matter is not YAML")
+
+
+def vocabulary_of(path):
+    try:
+        classes = yaml.safe_load(load(path))
+    except yaml.YAMLError:
+        fail(f"{path} is not YAML")
+    classes = classes.get("classes") if isinstance(classes, dict) else None
+    if not isinstance(classes, dict):
+        fail(f"{path} has no classes mapping")
+    return classes
+
+
+def paths(value):
+    """Whether value is a list of non-empty strings without tab, newline or carriage return."""
+    return isinstance(value, list) and all(isinstance(v, str) and v and not set(v) & set("\t\n\r") for v in value)
+
+
+def tokens(glob):
+    if glob.endswith("/**"):
+        return (*glob[:-2], ANY)
+    return tuple(STAR if c == "*" else QMARK if c == "?" else c for c in glob)
+
+
+def covers(entry, glob):
+    """Whether every path glob can name matches entry: each token of glob is absorbed by an entry token whose set holds
+    it. shortcut: sound but not complete (`?*` under `*?` reads as outside), so the orchestrator decides such a glob."""
+    a, g = tokens(entry), tokens(glob)
+
+    @functools.cache
+    def absorbs(i, j):
+        if i == len(a):
+            return j == len(g)
+        if a[i] is ANY:
+            return True
+        if a[i] is STAR:
+            return absorbs(i + 1, j) or (j < len(g) and g[j] is not ANY and g[j] != "/" and absorbs(i, j + 1))
+        if j == len(g):
+            return False
+        if a[i] is QMARK:
+            return (g[j] is QMARK or isinstance(g[j], str) and g[j] != "/") and absorbs(i + 1, j + 1)
+        return isinstance(g[j], str) and g[j] == a[i] and absorbs(i + 1, j + 1)
+
+    return absorbs(0, 0)
+
+
+def plan(root, task, cls):
+    o = Path(root) / ".orchestration"
+    if not (o / "learning/classes.yaml").is_file():
+        fail(".orchestration/learning/classes.yaml, the orchestrator's record of plan 024 P1-13, is absent from this checkout")
+    classes = vocabulary_of(o / "learning/classes.yaml")
+    if not paths([cls]):
+        fail(f"class {cls!r} is not a slug")
+    if cls not in classes:
+        fail(f"class {cls!r} is not in .orchestration/learning/classes.yaml")
+    entry = classes[cls] if isinstance(classes[cls], dict) else {}
+    remedy, radius = entry.get("remedy"), entry.get("blast_radius")
+    if not paths([remedy]) or not remedy.strip() or not paths(radius):
+        fail(f"class {cls!r} has no one-line remedy string or no blast_radius list of globs")
+    meta = front(o / f"tasks/{task}.md")
+    allowed = meta.get("allowed_files") if isinstance(meta, dict) else None
+    if not paths(allowed):
+        fail(f".orchestration/tasks/{task}.md has no allowed_files list of paths")
+    ledger = o / "decisions.tsv"
+    if not load(ledger).startswith("id\twhen\twhat\twhy\tbasis\tresult\n"):
+        fail(".orchestration/decisions.tsv does not start with its six-column header")
+    scope = [(glob, [a for a in allowed if covers(a, glob)]) for glob in radius]
+    outside = [glob for glob, by in scope if not by]
+    now = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
+    unknown = remedy == "unknown"
+    lines = [
+        f"# Re-plan skeleton: {task}",
+        "",
+        f"Written by `scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh {task} {cls}` at {now}. It is a proposal until the"
+        f" orchestrator replaces every `<fill>`; the task text `.orchestration/tasks/{task}.md` is unchanged.",
+        "",
+        "## Cause",
+        "",
+        f"- class: `{cls}`",
+        "- remedy: unknown (classes.yaml records no corrective procedure for this class)" if unknown else f"- remedy: {remedy}",
+        "",
+        "## Scope",
+        "",
+        *(
+            f"- `{glob}`: inside allowed_files ({', '.join(f'`{a}`' for a in by)})" if by else f"- `{glob}`: outside allowed_files"
+            for glob, by in scope
+        ),
+        *([] if scope else ["- none: the class's blast_radius is empty"]),
+        "",
+        "## Completion",
+        "",
+        "<fill: the observable result>, verified by <fill: test selectors>, with the evidence in <fill: evidence paths>.",
+        "",
+        "re-plan: specification — <fill>; design — <fill>; implementation — <fill>; verification — <fill>",
+        "",
+    ]
+    dest = o / f"tasks/{task}-replan.md"
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(dest, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL, 0o644)  # O_EXCL refuses any entry, a link included
+    except FileExistsError:
+        fail(f".orchestration/tasks/{task}-replan.md exists", 4)
+    with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as out:
+        out.write("\n".join(lines))
+    report = f"hold-playbook: wrote .orchestration/tasks/{task}-replan.md for class {cls}"
+    if unknown:
+        report += f" (remedy: unknown, blast_radius: [{', '.join(radius)}])"
+    if not outside:
+        print(f"{report}; every blast_radius glob is inside allowed_files")
+        return 0
+    row = [
+        f"D-{task}-replan",
+        now,
+        f"the re-plan of {task} needs files outside its allowed_files: " + ", ".join(f"`{g}`" for g in outside),
+        f"the blast_radius of class `{cls}` (classes.yaml) reaches beyond the task's allowed_files",
+        "hold-playbook",
+        "pending",
+    ]
+    try:
+        tail = ledger.read_bytes()[-1:]
+        with ledger.open("a", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as out:
+            out.write("" if tail == b"\n" else "\n")
+            csv.writer(out, delimiter="\t", lineterminator="\n").writerow(row)
+    except BaseException:
+        dest.unlink()  # a replan without its decision row would make every rerun exit 4 with no decision recorded
+        raise
+    print(f"{report}; outside allowed_files: {', '.join(outside)}; appended D-{task}-replan (pending) to decisions.tsv")
+    return 3
+
+
+def vocabulary(path, mistakes):
+    known = vocabulary_of(Path(path))
+    records = sorted(Path(mistakes).glob("M-*.md"))
+    if not records:
+        fail(f"{mistakes} holds no M-*.md record")
+    found = {}
+    for record in records:
+        meta = front(record)
+        slugs = meta.get("classes", []) if isinstance(meta, dict) else None
+        if not paths(slugs):
+            fail(f"{record.name}: classes is not a list of slugs")
+        for slug in slugs:
+            found.setdefault(slug, []).append(record.name)
+    missing = sorted(set(found) - set(known))
+    for slug in missing:
+        print(f"hold-playbook: class {slug} of {', '.join(found[slug])} is not in {path}", file=sys.stderr)
+    if missing:
+        return 2
+    print(f"hold-playbook: {path} holds the {len(found)} classes of {len(records)} mistake records")
+    return 0
+
+
+sys.exit(plan(*sys.argv[2:]) if sys.argv[1] == "plan" else vocabulary(*sys.argv[2:]))
+PY
diff --git a/tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py b/tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..14198f71
--- /dev/null
+++ b/tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py
@@ -0,0 +1,254 @@
+"""Exercise scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh (plan 024 P1-13) on fixture records.
+
+Every run uses a copy of the script in a temporary tree holding a fixture classes.yaml, task text and decision ledger;
+no test reads or writes the real .orchestration/ tree.
+"""
+
+import csv
+import datetime
+import json
+import re
+import shutil
+import subprocess
+import tempfile
+import unittest
+from pathlib import Path
+
+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+SCRIPT = ROOT / "scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh"
+TASK = "dotfiles-T999-fixture-a01"
+HEADER = "id\twhen\twhat\twhy\tbasis\tresult\n"
+OLD_ROW = 'D-old\t2026-10-19T00:00Z\t"an ""earlier"" decision"\tits reason\tplan 024 row\tdefault applied\n'
+ALLOWED = ["scripts/orchestrator/**", "tests/unit/test_*.py", f".orchestration/{TASK}/**"]
+CLASSES = {
+    "colored-json-parse": {
+        "remedy": "Read JSON from a --json file or NO_COLOR output, never from colored terminal text.",
+        "blast_radius": ["scripts/orchestrator/*.sh", "tests/unit/test_*.py"],
+    },
+    "concurrent-ff-clobber": {
+        "remedy": "Fast-forward main only from the one locked integration worktree.",
+        "blast_radius": ["scripts/orchestrator/ff-main.sh"],
+    },
+    "records-form": {
+        "remedy": "Generate the acceptance front matter with the record tool, never by hand.",
+        "blast_radius": [".orchestration/acceptance/*.md", "scripts/orchestrator/acceptance-front-matter.py"],
+    },
+    "no-remedy-yet": {"remedy": "unknown", "blast_radius": []},
+}
+REPLAN_LINE = "re-plan: specification — <fill>; design — <fill>; implementation — <fill>; verification — <fill>"
+
+
+class HoldPlaybookTests(unittest.TestCase):
+    def setUp(self):
+        tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
+        self.addCleanup(tmp.cleanup)
+        self.tree = Path(tmp.name)
+        self.script = self.tree / "scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh"
+        self.script.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
+        shutil.copy(SCRIPT, self.script)
+        self.ledger = self.tree / ".orchestration/decisions.tsv"
+        self.write(".orchestration/decisions.tsv", HEADER + OLD_ROW)
+        self.classes(CLASSES)
+        self.task(TASK, ALLOWED)
+
+    def write(self, rel, text):
+        path = self.tree / rel
+        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        path.write_text(text)
+        return path
+
+    def classes(self, classes):
+        self.write(".orchestration/learning/classes.yaml", json.dumps({"classes": classes}, indent=2))  # JSON is YAML
+
+    def task(self, task, allowed):
+        front = json.dumps({"format": 2, "task_id": task, "allowed_files": allowed})
+        return self.write(f".orchestration/tasks/{task}.md", f"---\n{front}\n---\n\n# {task}\n\n---\n\nprose\n")
+
+    def run_script(self, *args):
+        return subprocess.run(
+            ["bash", str(self.script), *args], check=False, cwd=self.tree, capture_output=True, text=True
+        )
+
+    def replan(self, task=TASK):
+        return (self.tree / f".orchestration/tasks/{task}-replan.md").read_text()
+
+    def rows(self):
+        with self.ledger.open(newline="") as ledger:
+            return list(csv.reader(ledger, delimiter="\t"))
+
+    def snapshot(self):
+        return {str(p.relative_to(self.tree)): p.read_bytes() for p in self.tree.rglob("*") if p.is_file()}
+
+    def test_three_classes_expand_into_replans(self):
+        expected = {
+            "colored-json-parse": (0, ["`scripts/orchestrator/*.sh`: inside allowed_files (`scripts/orchestrator/**`)",
+                                       "`tests/unit/test_*.py`: inside allowed_files (`tests/unit/test_*.py`)"]),
+            "concurrent-ff-clobber": (0, ["`scripts/orchestrator/ff-main.sh`: inside allowed_files (`scripts/orchestrator/**`)"]),
+            "records-form": (3, ["`.orchestration/acceptance/*.md`: outside allowed_files",
+                                 "`scripts/orchestrator/acceptance-front-matter.py`: inside allowed_files (`scripts/orchestrator/**`)"]),
+        }  # fmt: skip
+        for n, (cls, (code, scope)) in enumerate(expected.items()):
+            with self.subTest(cls=cls):
+                task = f"dotfiles-T999-class{n}-a01"
+                source = self.task(task, ALLOWED).read_bytes()
+                result = self.run_script(task, cls)
+                self.assertEqual(code, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+                text = self.replan(task)
+                self.assertIn(f"- class: `{cls}`\n", text)
+                self.assertIn(f"- remedy: {CLASSES[cls]['remedy']}\n", text)
+                self.assertIn("".join(f"- {line}\n" for line in scope), text)
+                self.assertIn("<fill: the observable result>", text)
+                self.assertIn("<fill: test selectors>", text)
+                self.assertIn("<fill: evidence paths>", text)
+                self.assertIn(f"\n{REPLAN_LINE}\n", text)
+                self.assertEqual(source, (self.tree / f".orchestration/tasks/{task}.md").read_bytes())
+
+    def test_uncovered_scope_appends_pending_decision(self):
+        start = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.UTC).replace(microsecond=0)
+        result = self.run_script(TASK, "records-form")
+        self.assertEqual(3, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+        text = self.ledger.read_text()
+        self.assertTrue(text.startswith(HEADER + OLD_ROW))
+        rows = self.rows()
+        self.assertEqual(3, len(rows))
+        self.assertEqual(6, len(rows[2]))
+        row = dict(zip(HEADER.split(), rows[2]))
+        self.assertEqual(f"D-{TASK}-replan", row["id"])
+        self.assertRegex(row["when"], r"^\d{4}-\d\d-\d\dT\d\d:\d\d:\d\dZ$")
+        when = datetime.datetime.strptime(row["when"], "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S%z")
+        self.assertTrue(start <= when <= datetime.datetime.now(datetime.UTC), row["when"])  # UTC, not local
+        self.assertIn(".orchestration/acceptance/*.md", row["what"])
+        self.assertNotIn("acceptance-front-matter.py", row["what"])
+        self.assertIn("records-form", row["why"])
+        self.assertEqual(("hold-playbook", "pending"), (row["basis"], row["result"]))
+        self.assertNotRegex(text[len(HEADER + OLD_ROW) :].lower(), r"default|deadline")
+
+    def test_unknown_class_exits_2(self):
+        self.classes({**CLASSES, "a\tb": CLASSES["records-form"]})  # a key, but a tab would split the ledger row
+        before = self.snapshot()
+        for cls in ("no-such-class", "a\tb"):
+            with self.subTest(cls=cls):
+                result = self.run_script(TASK, cls)
+                self.assertEqual(2, result.returncode)
+                self.assertIn(repr(cls), result.stderr)
+                self.assertEqual(before, self.snapshot())
+
+    def test_an_absent_vocabulary_is_refused_as_the_orchestrator_record(self):
+        # Revise 2 (the local review of 9f5eaee): a clean checkout has no classes.yaml, the orchestrator's record.
+        (self.tree / ".orchestration/learning/classes.yaml").unlink()
+        before = self.snapshot()
+        result = self.run_script(TASK, "records-form")
+        self.assertEqual(2, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+        self.assertIn(".orchestration/learning/classes.yaml", result.stderr)
+        self.assertIn("the orchestrator's record of plan 024 P1-13", result.stderr)
+        self.assertNotIn("Traceback", result.stderr)
+        self.assertEqual(before, self.snapshot())
+
+    def test_existing_replan_exits_4(self):
+        self.assertEqual(3, self.run_script(TASK, "records-form").returncode)
+        before = self.snapshot()
+        result = self.run_script(TASK, "records-form")  # a repeated out-of-scope run
+        self.assertEqual(4, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+        self.assertEqual(before, self.snapshot())
+        self.assertEqual(3, len(self.rows()))
+        for task, make in (("dotfiles-T999-kept-a01", "file"), ("dotfiles-T999-link-a01", "link")):
+            with self.subTest(destination=make):
+                self.task(task, ALLOWED)
+                dest = self.tree / f".orchestration/tasks/{task}-replan.md"
+                if make == "file":
+                    dest.write_text("kept\n")
+                else:
+                    dest.symlink_to(self.tree / "elsewhere.md")  # dangling: following it would create the target
+                before = self.snapshot()
+                self.assertEqual(4, self.run_script(task, "records-form").returncode)
+                self.assertEqual(before, self.snapshot())
+                self.assertFalse((self.tree / "elsewhere.md").exists())
+
+    def test_invalid_task_id_is_refused(self):
+        self.task("x", ALLOWED)
+        before = self.snapshot()
+        for task in ("", "../x", "a/b", "-x", ".x", "a b", "x\n", f"{TASK};true"):
+            with self.subTest(task=task):
+                result = self.run_script(task, "records-form")
+                self.assertEqual(2, result.returncode)
+                self.assertIn("task id", result.stderr)
+                self.assertEqual(before, self.snapshot())
+
+    def test_scope_comparison_covers_the_whole_glob(self):
+        allowed = [
+            "scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh",
+            "tests/**",
+            "lib/*",
+            "ab?cd",
+            "docs/a.md",
+            f".orchestration/{TASK}/**",
+        ]
+        radius = ["scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh", "tests/unit/test_*.py", "scripts/**", "lib/**", "docs/*.md"]
+        radius += ["lib/*/x.md", "ab/cd"]  # neither `*` nor `?` of an entry takes a `/`
+        self.classes({"scope-mix": {"remedy": "Fix it within scope.", "blast_radius": radius}})
+        self.task(TASK, allowed)
+        self.write("lib/x", "")
+        self.write("docs/a.md", "")  # every existing match of lib/** and docs/*.md is allowed, the scopes are not
+        result = self.run_script(TASK, "scope-mix")
+        self.assertEqual(3, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+        scope = [
+            "`scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh`: inside allowed_files (`scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh`)",
+            "`tests/unit/test_*.py`: inside allowed_files (`tests/**`)",
+            "`scripts/**`: outside allowed_files",
+            "`lib/**`: outside allowed_files",
+            "`docs/*.md`: outside allowed_files",
+            "`lib/*/x.md`: outside allowed_files",
+            "`ab/cd`: outside allowed_files",
+        ]
+        self.assertIn("".join(f"- {line}\n" for line in scope), self.replan())
+        what = self.rows()[2][2]
+        self.assertEqual(
+            ["ab/cd", "docs/*.md", "lib/**", "lib/*/x.md", "scripts/**"], sorted(g for g in radius if g in what)
+        )
+
+    def test_record_text_is_never_executed(self):
+        forged = "r\n\n## Completion\n\n" + REPLAN_LINE.replace("<fill>", "done")  # would forge the filled sections
+        self.classes({"forged": {"remedy": forged, "blast_radius": []}})
+        before = self.snapshot()
+        self.assertEqual(2, self.run_script(TASK, "forged").returncode)
+        self.assertEqual(before, self.snapshot())
+        remedy = "$(touch ran-remedy) `touch ran-tick`; touch ran-semi"
+        glob = "$(touch ran-glob)/*.md"
+        self.classes({"literal": {"remedy": remedy, "blast_radius": [glob, "`touch ran-glob2`"]}})
+        self.task(TASK, ["$(touch ran-allowed)", f".orchestration/{TASK}/**"])
+        result = self.run_script(TASK, "literal")
+        self.assertEqual(3, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+        self.assertEqual([], sorted(str(p) for p in self.tree.rglob("ran-*")))
+        text = self.replan()
+        self.assertIn(f"- remedy: {remedy}\n", text)
+        self.assertIn(f"- `{glob}`: outside allowed_files\n", text)
+        self.assertIn(glob, self.rows()[2][2])
+
+    def test_known_class_without_remedy_is_reported(self):
+        before = self.ledger.read_bytes()
+        result = self.run_script(TASK, "no-remedy-yet")
+        self.assertEqual(0, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+        self.assertIn("remedy: unknown", result.stdout)
+        text = self.replan()
+        self.assertIn("- remedy: unknown (classes.yaml records no corrective procedure for this class)\n", text)
+        self.assertIn("- none: the class's blast_radius is empty\n", text)
+        self.assertIn(f"\n{REPLAN_LINE}\n", text)
+        self.assertEqual(before, self.ledger.read_bytes())
+
+    def test_vocabulary_covers_mistake_classes(self):
+        mistakes = self.tree / "mistakes"
+        self.write("mistakes/M-1.md", "---\nid: M-1\nclasses: [colored-json-parse, records-form]\n---\n\nbody\n")
+        self.write("mistakes/M-2.md", "---\nid: M-2\nclasses:\n  - records-form\n  - concurrent-ff-clobber\n---\n")
+        self.write("mistakes/notes.md", "---\nclasses: [not-a-record]\n---\n")
+        vocabulary = self.tree / ".orchestration/learning/classes.yaml"
+        result = self.run_script("--check-vocabulary", str(vocabulary), str(mistakes))
+        self.assertEqual(0, result.returncode, result.stderr)
+        self.write("mistakes/M-3.md", "---\nid: M-3\nclasses: [new-class-b, records-form, new-class-a]\n---\n")
+        result = self.run_script("--check-vocabulary", str(vocabulary), str(mistakes))
+        self.assertEqual(2, result.returncode)
+        self.assertEqual(["new-class-a", "new-class-b"], re.findall(r"class (\S+) of M-3\.md is not in", result.stderr))
+        self.assertNotIn("not-a-record", result.stderr)
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    unittest.main()
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# @file hold-playbook.sh
     3	# @brief Expand a class's remedy into a replan skeleton and record a pending decision for out-of-scope globs.
     4	# @description
     5	#   Plan 024 P1-13 (item (13) of 024-design-programs.md, the class playbook of 024-design-quality.md 5.13). Reads the
     6	#   class's `remedy:` and `blast_radius:` from `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` and the task's `allowed_files`
     7	#   from the first YAML front-matter document of `.orchestration/tasks/<task>.md`, both with yaml.safe_load, and
     8	#   creates `.orchestration/tasks/<task>-replan.md`: the cause class, the remedy, each blast-radius glob with the
     9	#   allowed_files entries that cover it (or marked outside allowed_files), a Completion sentence and a four-field
    10	#   `re-plan:` line of `<fill>` placeholders for the orchestrator. The root is the checkout this script lives in.
    11	#   A glob is covered by an entry when every path the glob can name, existing or not, matches the entry under
    12	#   regime-ci.py's allowed(): a trailing `/**` takes any path under its literal prefix, and elsewhere `*` and `?`
    13	#   stay within one path segment. When a glob is uncovered, one six-column row `D-<task>-replan` (basis
    14	#   `hold-playbook`, result `pending`, no default) is appended to `.orchestration/decisions.tsv`.
    15	#   Record text is only read and written as data; the script never edits the task text and never dispatches.
    16	#   With `--check-vocabulary`, it lists every class slug in the `classes:` front-matter lists of `M-*.md` in the
    17	#   mistakes directory that the class file lacks.
    18	# @arg $1 string Task id, matching `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$`.
    19	# @arg $2 string Class slug, a key of classes.yaml's `classes` mapping.
    20	# @option --check-vocabulary <classes.yaml> <mistakes-dir> Check the class file against the mistake records instead.
    21	# @exitcode 0 The replan is written and every glob is inside allowed_files; or the vocabulary covers the records.
    22	# @exitcode 2 Usage error, an invalid task id, a class not in classes.yaml, malformed or missing records; nothing is
    23	#   written. With --check-vocabulary, also a class slug of the records that the class file lacks.
    24	# @exitcode 3 The replan is written and the pending decision row is appended: a glob is outside allowed_files.
    25	# @exitcode 4 The replan destination already exists (a file, a directory or a link); nothing is written.
    26	# @example
    27	#   scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01 records-form
    28	#   scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh --check-vocabulary .orchestration/learning/classes.yaml \
    29	#       .orchestration/learning/mistakes
    30	set -euo pipefail
    31	
    32	usage() {
    33	    echo "usage: hold-playbook.sh <task> <class> | hold-playbook.sh --check-vocabulary <classes.yaml> <mistakes-dir>" >&2
    34	    exit 2
    35	}
    36	if (($# == 3)) && [[ $1 == --check-vocabulary ]]; then
    37	    set -- vocabulary "$2" "$3"
    38	elif (($# == 2)); then
    39	    [[ $1 =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$ ]] || {
    40	        echo "hold-playbook: invalid task id; refused" >&2
    41	        exit 2
    42	    }
    43	    set -- plan "$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)" "$1" "$2"
    44	else
    45	    usage
    46	fi
    47	exec uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -I - "$@" << 'PY'
    48	import csv
    49	import datetime
    50	import functools
    51	import os
    52	import sys
    53	from pathlib import Path
    54	
    55	import yaml
    56	
    57	STAR, QMARK, ANY = object(), object(), object()
    58	
    59	
    60	def fail(message, code=2):
    61	    print(f"hold-playbook: {message}; refused", file=sys.stderr)
    62	    sys.exit(code)
    63	
    64	
    65	def load(path):
    66	    try:
    67	        return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    68	    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as error:
    69	        fail(f"cannot read {path}: {error.__class__.__name__}")
    70	
    71	
    72	def front(path):
    73	    """The first YAML front-matter document: the lines between the file's first `---` line and the next."""
    74	    lines = load(path).split("\n")
    75	    if lines[0] != "---" or "---" not in lines[1:]:
    76	        fail(f"{path} has no front matter")
    77	    try:
    78	        return yaml.safe_load("\n".join(lines[1 : lines.index("---", 1)]))
    79	    except yaml.YAMLError:
    80	        fail(f"{path}: the front matter is not YAML")
    81	
    82	
    83	def vocabulary_of(path):
    84	    try:
    85	        classes = yaml.safe_load(load(path))
    86	    except yaml.YAMLError:
    87	        fail(f"{path} is not YAML")
    88	    classes = classes.get("classes") if isinstance(classes, dict) else None
    89	    if not isinstance(classes, dict):
    90	        fail(f"{path} has no classes mapping")
    91	    return classes
    92	
    93	
    94	def paths(value):
    95	    """Whether value is a list of non-empty strings without tab, newline or carriage return."""
    96	    return isinstance(value, list) and all(isinstance(v, str) and v and not set(v) & set("\t\n\r") for v in value)
    97	
    98	
    99	def tokens(glob):
   100	    if glob.endswith("/**"):
   101	        return (*glob[:-2], ANY)
   102	    return tuple(STAR if c == "*" else QMARK if c == "?" else c for c in glob)
   103	
   104	
   105	def covers(entry, glob):
   106	    """Whether every path glob can name matches entry: each token of glob is absorbed by an entry token whose set holds
   107	    it. shortcut: sound but not complete (`?*` under `*?` reads as outside), so the orchestrator decides such a glob."""
   108	    a, g = tokens(entry), tokens(glob)
   109	
   110	    @functools.cache
   111	    def absorbs(i, j):
   112	        if i == len(a):
   113	            return j == len(g)
   114	        if a[i] is ANY:
   115	            return True
   116	        if a[i] is STAR:
   117	            return absorbs(i + 1, j) or (j < len(g) and g[j] is not ANY and g[j] != "/" and absorbs(i, j + 1))
   118	        if j == len(g):
   119	            return False
   120	        if a[i] is QMARK:
   121	            return (g[j] is QMARK or isinstance(g[j], str) and g[j] != "/") and absorbs(i + 1, j + 1)
   122	        return isinstance(g[j], str) and g[j] == a[i] and absorbs(i + 1, j + 1)
   123	
   124	    return absorbs(0, 0)
   125	
   126	
   127	def plan(root, task, cls):
   128	    o = Path(root) / ".orchestration"
   129	    if not (o / "learning/classes.yaml").is_file():
   130	        fail(".orchestration/learning/classes.yaml, the orchestrator's record of plan 024 P1-13, is absent from this checkout")
   131	    classes = vocabulary_of(o / "learning/classes.yaml")
   132	    if not paths([cls]):
   133	        fail(f"class {cls!r} is not a slug")
   134	    if cls not in classes:
   135	        fail(f"class {cls!r} is not in .orchestration/learning/classes.yaml")
   136	    entry = classes[cls] if isinstance(classes[cls], dict) else {}
   137	    remedy, radius = entry.get("remedy"), entry.get("blast_radius")
   138	    if not paths([remedy]) or not remedy.strip() or not paths(radius):
   139	        fail(f"class {cls!r} has no one-line remedy string or no blast_radius list of globs")
   140	    meta = front(o / f"tasks/{task}.md")
   141	    allowed = meta.get("allowed_files") if isinstance(meta, dict) else None
   142	    if not paths(allowed):
   143	        fail(f".orchestration/tasks/{task}.md has no allowed_files list of paths")
   144	    ledger = o / "decisions.tsv"
   145	    if not load(ledger).startswith("id\twhen\twhat\twhy\tbasis\tresult\n"):
   146	        fail(".orchestration/decisions.tsv does not start with its six-column header")
   147	    scope = [(glob, [a for a in allowed if covers(a, glob)]) for glob in radius]
   148	    outside = [glob for glob, by in scope if not by]
   149	    now = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
   150	    unknown = remedy == "unknown"
   151	    lines = [
   152	        f"# Re-plan skeleton: {task}",
   153	        "",
   154	        f"Written by `scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh {task} {cls}` at {now}. It is a proposal until the"
   155	        f" orchestrator replaces every `<fill>`; the task text `.orchestration/tasks/{task}.md` is unchanged.",
   156	        "",
   157	        "## Cause",
   158	        "",
   159	        f"- class: `{cls}`",
   160	        "- remedy: unknown (classes.yaml records no corrective procedure for this class)" if unknown else f"- remedy: {remedy}",
   161	        "",
   162	        "## Scope",
   163	        "",
   164	        *(
   165	            f"- `{glob}`: inside allowed_files ({', '.join(f'`{a}`' for a in by)})" if by else f"- `{glob}`: outside allowed_files"
   166	            for glob, by in scope
   167	        ),
   168	        *([] if scope else ["- none: the class's blast_radius is empty"]),
   169	        "",
   170	        "## Completion",
   171	        "",
   172	        "<fill: the observable result>, verified by <fill: test selectors>, with the evidence in <fill: evidence paths>.",
   173	        "",

# Report: dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01

- Seat: `claude-standard-dot-a011` in `.claude/worktrees/worker-d`. After a single-command `git fetch origin main`, the branch `tools/hold-playbook-a01` was created with `--no-track` from `origin/main` `b22a5580`. The previous branch, `tools/regime-ci-followup-a01` (PR #425, pending integration), was pushed and clean before the switch.
- Task file: `e33f0aa1…`, copied byte-identical to `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/task.md` (`5e719af3`). The task-consistency check passes (validation §1).
- The orchestrator note governs item 1. `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` is the orchestrator's record, outside this task's `allowed_files`, so this branch neither adds nor edits it. Item 1 is read as the format the script consumes: a top-level `classes:` mapping of slug to `remedy:` (a string, `unknown` when no procedure is known) and `blast_radius:` (a list of globs). The pinned-vocabulary check is the script's `--check-vocabulary <classes.yaml> <mistakes-dir>` mode, tested with fixtures only. No test reads the real `.orchestration/learning/` tree.

## What the script does

`scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh <task> <class>` is a bash wrapper with shdoc comments. It checks the task id against `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$` before any path is built. Then it runs one embedded Python helper through `uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -I -`, which receives the record values only as argv and stdin text. Record text is never seen by a shell.

- **Inputs.** The helper reads `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` and the first YAML front-matter document of `.orchestration/tasks/<task>.md`, both with `yaml.safe_load`. The front matter is the lines between the file's first `---` line and the next. The root is the checkout the script lives in.
- **Refusals (exit 2, nothing written):**
  - a class absent from `classes.yaml`, with the supplied slug named, or a class argument holding a tab, newline or carriage return;
  - a class entry without a one-line remedy string (a newline could forge the replan's own sections), or without a `blast_radius` list of globs, each free of tab, newline and carriage return;
  - a task without an `allowed_files` list;
  - a decision ledger that does not start with its six-column header;
  - an unreadable file.
- **The replan.** `.orchestration/tasks/<task>-replan.md` is created with `O_CREAT | O_EXCL`. Any existing entry, including a directory or a dangling link, exits 4 before the ledger is touched. The replan holds:
  - the cause class and the remedy (`unknown` is spelled out as such);
  - every blast-radius glob, with the `allowed_files` entries that cover it or `outside allowed_files`;
  - a Completion sentence with `<fill: the observable result>`, `<fill: test selectors>` and `<fill: evidence paths>`;
  - the four-field line `re-plan: specification — <fill>; design — <fill>; implementation — <fill>; verification — <fill>`, the keys of the `replans` object in `.orchestration/acceptance/dotfiles-T133-permission-guard-gate-a04.md`.
- **Exit 0.** Every glob is covered, including the empty blast radius of an unknown remedy. Exit 0 establishes scope coverage only.
- **Exit 3.** Some glob is uncovered. Exactly one row is appended to `.orchestration/decisions.tsv` with Python's csv writer (tab-delimited, quoted as the ledger's existing rows are). Its six fields:
  - `id`: `D-<task>-replan`;
  - `when`: the UTC time to the second;
  - `what`: names the uncovered globs;
  - `why`: names the class;
  - `basis`: `hold-playbook`;
  - `result`: `pending`. No default and no deadline are written.
  - If the append fails, the helper removes the replan it just created, so a rerun is not refused with exit 4 while no decision was recorded.
- **Never:** it never edits the source task, an existing replan, an acceptance record or the learning records, and it never dispatches.

### Coverage of a glob

A blast-radius glob is covered by an `allowed_files` entry when every path the glob can name, existing or not, matches the entry. Matching follows `regime-ci.py`'s `allowed()`, which the regime job enforces: a trailing `/**` takes any path under its literal prefix, and elsewhere `*` and `?` stay within one path segment.

`covers()` decides this on the two patterns themselves, never by expanding files. Each token of the glob must be absorbed by an entry token whose set holds it:

- a literal, by the same literal;
- one literal other than `/`, or one `?`, by `?`;
- any run of literals other than `/`, `?`s and `*`s, by `*`;
- everything that remains, a trailing `/**` included, by a trailing `/**`.

This is sound. It is not complete: for example, `?*` under `*?` reads as outside. That limit errs toward a pending decision, and the code carries a `shortcut:` comment saying so.

Validation §5 shows that two wrong ways of comparing scope turn the whole-glob test red:

- matching the pattern string as a filename (`fnmatch`);
- expanding only the files that exist.

| Item 2: an uncovered glob appends one pending six-field row with the UTC time, existing rows kept, no default | `test_uncovered_scope_appends_pending_decision` | no decision row; a default in the row; local time in the row (red where the local zone is not UTC) |

| Item 2: record text stays text, and a multi-line remedy is refused | `test_record_text_is_never_executed` | record text run by a shell; a multi-line remedy accepted |

# Learning: dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01

1. Whether a glob covers a scope is a question about two patterns, not about files. Expanding the files that exist, or matching one pattern string as a filename against the other, both report coverage that the regime would refuse for a later file. Mechanism: decide containment on the patterns' tokens under the same matcher the regime enforces (`regime-ci.py`'s `allowed()`), and keep one test per trap. Each trap is a broader glob whose every existing match is allowed, and §5 turns it red with the two wrong comparisons.
2. A Check command quoted without flags can disagree with the repository's formatter configuration. Bare `shfmt -d` follows `.editorconfig`, which here sets `indent_size = 4` without `indent_style`, and it then wants tabs in every script. Mechanism: run the command verbatim, reproduce its result on unchanged files at the base, run the repository's own invocation (`make format`'s flags), and report the mismatch rather than reformatting against the house style.
3. A test-first commit whose script does not exist yet is red only by an error in setUp, which says nothing about the tests' strength. Mechanism: a mutation table at the code head. Each test is paired with a plausible wrong implementation that turns it red, so every requirement row names the defect its test catches.

# Report: dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01

- Seat: `claude-standard-dot-a011` in `.claude/worktrees/worker-d`. After a single-command `git fetch origin main`, the branch `tools/hold-playbook-a01` was created with `--no-track` from `origin/main` `b22a5580`. The previous branch, `tools/regime-ci-followup-a01` (PR #425, pending integration), was pushed and clean before the switch.
- Task file: `e33f0aa1…`, copied byte-identical to `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01/task.md` (`5e719af3`). The task-consistency check passes (validation §1).
- The orchestrator note governs item 1. `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` is the orchestrator's record, outside this task's `allowed_files`, so this branch neither adds nor edits it. Item 1 is read as the format the script consumes: a top-level `classes:` mapping of slug to `remedy:` (a string, `unknown` when no procedure is known) and `blast_radius:` (a list of globs). The pinned-vocabulary check is the script's `--check-vocabulary <classes.yaml> <mistakes-dir>` mode, tested with fixtures only. No test reads the real `.orchestration/learning/` tree.

## What the script does

`scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh <task> <class>` is a bash wrapper with shdoc comments. It checks the task id against `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$` before any path is built. Then it runs one embedded Python helper through `uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -I -`, which receives the record values only as argv and stdin text. Record text is never seen by a shell.

- **Inputs.** The helper reads `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` and the first YAML front-matter document of `.orchestration/tasks/<task>.md`, both with `yaml.safe_load`. The front matter is the lines between the file's first `---` line and the next. The root is the checkout the script lives in.
- **Refusals (exit 2, nothing written):**
  - a class absent from `classes.yaml`, with the supplied slug named, or a class argument holding a tab, newline or carriage return;
  - a class entry without a one-line remedy string (a newline could forge the replan's own sections), or without a `blast_radius` list of globs, each free of tab, newline and carriage return;
  - a task without an `allowed_files` list;
  - a decision ledger that does not start with its six-column header;
  - an unreadable file.
- **The replan.** `.orchestration/tasks/<task>-replan.md` is created with `O_CREAT | O_EXCL`. Any existing entry, including a directory or a dangling link, exits 4 before the ledger is touched. The replan holds:
  - the cause class and the remedy (`unknown` is spelled out as such);
  - every blast-radius glob, with the `allowed_files` entries that cover it or `outside allowed_files`;
  - a Completion sentence with `<fill: the observable result>`, `<fill: test selectors>` and `<fill: evidence paths>`;
  - the four-field line `re-plan: specification — <fill>; design — <fill>; implementation — <fill>; verification — <fill>`, the keys of the `replans` object in `.orchestration/acceptance/dotfiles-T133-permission-guard-gate-a04.md`.
- **Exit 0.** Every glob is covered, including the empty blast radius of an unknown remedy. Exit 0 establishes scope coverage only.
- **Exit 3.** Some glob is uncovered. Exactly one row is appended to `.orchestration/decisions.tsv` with Python's csv writer (tab-delimited, quoted as the ledger's existing rows are). Its six fields:
  - `id`: `D-<task>-replan`;
  - `when`: the UTC time to the second;
  - `what`: names the uncovered globs;
  - `why`: names the class;
  - `basis`: `hold-playbook`;
  - `result`: `pending`. No default and no deadline are written.
  - If the append fails, the helper removes the replan it just created, so a rerun is not refused with exit 4 while no decision was recorded.
- **Never:** it never edits the source task, an existing replan, an acceptance record or the learning records, and it never dispatches.

### Coverage of a glob

A blast-radius glob is covered by an `allowed_files` entry when every path the glob can name, existing or not, matches the entry. Matching follows `regime-ci.py`'s `allowed()`, which the regime job enforces: a trailing `/**` takes any path under its literal prefix, and elsewhere `*` and `?` stay within one path segment.

`covers()` decides this on the two patterns themselves, never by expanding files. Each token of the glob must be absorbed by an entry token whose set holds it:

- a literal, by the same literal;
- one literal other than `/`, or one `?`, by `?`;
- any run of literals other than `/`, `?`s and `*`s, by `*`;
- everything that remains, a trailing `/**` included, by a trailing `/**`.

This is sound. It is not complete: for example, `?*` under `*?` reads as outside. That limit errs toward a pending decision, and the code carries a `shortcut:` comment saying so.

Validation §5 shows that two wrong ways of comparing scope turn the whole-glob test red:

- matching the pattern string as a filename (`fnmatch`);
- expanding only the files that exist.

## Files requirements and their tests

All selectors are in `tests/unit/test_hold_playbook.py::HoldPlaybookTests`. Each was red at `6a72398c`, before the script existed, and is green at the code head (validation §3). §5 names the wrong implementations each one catches: 15 mutations, each red, with an unmutated green baseline.

| Requirement | Test | Wrong implementation it catches (§5) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Item 1: the pinned vocabulary (the orchestrator note's `--check-vocabulary` mode); exit 2 lists every missing slug | `test_vocabulary_covers_mistake_classes` | missing classes not reported |
| Item 1: `remedy: unknown` and `blast_radius: []` reported, coverage exit | `test_known_class_without_remedy_is_reported` | an unknown remedy not reported |
| Item 2: a replan per class, with the remedy, scope, Completion placeholders and four `re-plan:` fields; the source task unchanged | `test_three_classes_expand_into_replans` (`colored-json-parse`, `concurrent-ff-clobber`, `records-form`) | coverage by existing files only |
| Item 2: an uncovered glob appends one pending six-field row with the UTC time, existing rows kept, no default | `test_uncovered_scope_appends_pending_decision` | no decision row; a default in the row; local time in the row (red where the local zone is not UTC) |
| Item 2: an unknown class exits 2, names the slug, and writes nothing; so does a class key holding a tab | `test_unknown_class_exits_2` | an unknown class not refused; a class with a tab accepted |
| Item 2: an existing destination exits 4, kept; a repeated out-of-scope run appends no second row; a dangling link is not followed | `test_existing_replan_exits_4` | an existing replan overwritten |
| Item 2: an invalid task id exits 2 before any path | `test_invalid_task_id_is_refused` | the task id not checked |
| Item 2: coverage of the whole glob (an exact scope, a scope under an allowed directory glob, broader scopes, and an entry `*` or `?` never taking a `/`) | `test_scope_comparison_covers_the_whole_glob` | coverage by the pattern string as a filename; coverage by existing files only; an entry `*` taking a `/`; an entry `?` taking a `/` |
| Item 2: record text stays text, and a multi-line remedy is refused | `test_record_text_is_never_executed` | record text run by a shell; a multi-line remedy accepted |

## Class-source attribution and counts

This branch adds no class entry: the orchestrator seeded `classes.yaml` (untracked in the main checkout at this run) and attributes each entry with its `records:` list. The three expansion classes in the tests are fixtures; the task names them, and their remedies and globs were written for the tests.

The read-only smoke run of the completed script against the main checkout counted 80 `M-*.md` records and 97 distinct class slugs, all present in the seeded file (validation §8, exit 0). The orchestrator runs that command again at acceptance.

## The Check

- `uv run --no-project --with pyyaml python -m unittest tests.unit.test_hold_playbook -v`: 9 tests OK at the code head, with no warning (§3). Red at `6a72398c`: 9 errors (§3).
- `shellcheck scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh`: exit 0 (§4).
- `shfmt -d scripts/orchestrator/hold-playbook.sh`: exit 1. This is an inherited mismatch between the Check text and the repository's style (§4).
  - `.editorconfig` sets `indent_size = 4` and `space_redirects = true` for `*.sh` but no `indent_style`. shfmt v3.14.1 then wants tabs, so the bare command flags every four-space script, for example `orch-audit.sh`, `dispatch.sh` and `ci-rerun.sh` on the unchanged base.
  - With the repository's own flags, `shfmt --indent 4 --space-redirects --diff` (`make format`), the script is clean.
  - I did not reformat the script with tabs, which would break the repository style. I report the mismatch instead.
- `make format`: exit 2 (§7). Its whole-tree shfmt step flags 12 files, all unchanged from `b22a5580`. ruff (66 files) and prettier are clean, and `ruff check` passes on the new test module.
- Caps (§6): 231 changed lines outside `tests/` and `.orchestration/` (cap 350), and 243 under `tests/` (cap 400).
- No local Bats ran. Every script execution in the tests uses a temporary fixture tree.

## Threat table

Every row is `residual`, and the change keeps each control:

- The helper reads records only through `yaml.safe_load` and `read_text`. It writes only the replan and, conditionally, one ledger row. It runs no command derived from records, which the literal-data test and the shell mutation in §5 check.
- The tests copy the script into temporary trees.

## 9. Revise 2: an absent class vocabulary is refused by name

The chain's local review of the update-branch head `9f5eaeec` (`.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-hold-playbook-a01-local-review-9f5eaee.md`) found a P1. On a clean checkout, `.orchestration/learning/classes.yaml` is absent at both the head and the base, because it is the orchestrator's record and lands with the boundary records PR (C2). So every valid run exits 2 with only `cannot read …: FileNotFoundError`. `c04fe52d` refuses through `fail()` before any read, naming the file as the orchestrator's record of plan 024 P1-13: exit 2, no traceback, nothing written. Nothing else changes. `PREV` is `9f5eaeec`, `RED` is `9bac489c` (the new test on `PREV`'s script), `FIX` is `c04fe52d` and `TEST` is the new test.

   210	def vocabulary(path, mistakes):
   211	    known = vocabulary_of(Path(path))
   212	    records = sorted(Path(mistakes).glob("M-*.md"))
   213	    if not records:
   214	        fail(f"{mistakes} holds no M-*.md record")
   215	    found = {}
   216	    for record in records:
   217	        meta = front(record)
   218	        slugs = meta.get("classes", []) if isinstance(meta, dict) else None
   219	        if not paths(slugs):
   220	            fail(f"{record.name}: classes is not a list of slugs")
   221	        for slug in slugs:
   222	            found.setdefault(slug, []).append(record.name)
   223	    missing = sorted(set(found) - set(known))
   224	    for slug in missing:
   225	        print(f"hold-playbook: class {slug} of {', '.join(found[slug])} is not in {path}", file=sys.stderr)
   226	    if missing:
   227	        return 2
   228	    print(f"hold-playbook: {path} holds the {len(found)} classes of {len(records)} mistake records")
   229	    return 0

   238	    def test_vocabulary_covers_mistake_classes(self):
   239	        mistakes = self.tree / "mistakes"
   240	        self.write("mistakes/M-1.md", "---\nid: M-1\nclasses: [colored-json-parse, records-form]\n---\n\nbody\n")
   241	        self.write("mistakes/M-2.md", "---\nid: M-2\nclasses:\n  - records-form\n  - concurrent-ff-clobber\n---\n")
   242	        self.write("mistakes/notes.md", "---\nclasses: [not-a-record]\n---\n")
   243	        vocabulary = self.tree / ".orchestration/learning/classes.yaml"
   244	        result = self.run_script("--check-vocabulary", str(vocabulary), str(mistakes))
   245	        self.assertEqual(0, result.returncode, result.stderr)
   246	        self.write("mistakes/M-3.md", "---\nid: M-3\nclasses: [new-class-b, records-form, new-class-a]\n---\n")
   247	        result = self.run_script("--check-vocabulary", str(vocabulary), str(mistakes))
   248	        self.assertEqual(2, result.returncode)
   249	        self.assertEqual(["new-class-a", "new-class-b"], re.findall(r"class (\S+) of M-3\.md is not in", result.stderr))
   250	        self.assertNotIn("not-a-record", result.stderr)

1-Run a command or script.
2-
3:Ensures that the command runs in a Python environment.
4-
5-When used with a file ending in `.py` or an HTTP(S) URL, the file will be treated as a script and
6:run with a Python interpreter, i.e., `uv run file.py` is equivalent to `uv run python file.py`. For
7-URLs, the script is temporarily downloaded before execution. If the script contains inline
8:dependency metadata, it will be installed into an isolated, ephemeral environment. When used with
9-`-`, the input will be read from stdin, and treated as a Python script.
10-
11:When used in a project, the project environment will be created and updated before invoking the
12-command.
13-
14:When used outside a project, if a virtual environment can be found in the current directory or a
15:parent directory, the command will be run in that environment. Otherwise, the command will be run in
16:the environment of the discovered interpreter.
17-
18-When running a script, the project or workspace is discovered from the script's directory.
19-Otherwise, the project or workspace is discovered from the current working directory.
20-
21-Arguments following the command (or script) are not interpreted as arguments to uv. All options to
22-uv must be provided before the command, e.g., `uv run --verbose foo`. A `--` can be used to separate
23:the command from uv options for clarity, e.g., `uv run --python 3.12 -- python`.
24-
25-Usage: uv run [OPTIONS] [COMMAND]
26-
27-Options:
28-      --extra <EXTRA>
--
94-  -m, --module
95-          Run a Python module.
96-
97:          Equivalent to `python -m <module>`.
98-
99-      --no-editable
100-          Install any editable dependencies, including the project and any workspace members, as
101-          non-editable.
102-
--
108-      --exact
109-          Perform an exact sync, removing extraneous packages.
110-
111:          When enabled, uv will remove any extraneous packages from the environment. By default, `uv
112-          run` will make the minimum necessary changes to satisfy the requirements.
113-
114-      --env-file <ENV_FILE>
115:          Load environment variables from a `.env` file.
116-
117-          Can be provided multiple times, with subsequent files overriding values defined in
118-          previous files.
119-
120-          [env: UV_ENV_FILE=]
121-
122-      --no-env-file
123:          Avoid reading environment variables from a `.env` file.
124-
125-  -w, --with <WITH>
126-          Run with the given packages installed.
127-
128-          When used in a project, these dependencies will be layered on top of the project
129:          environment in a separate, ephemeral environment. These dependencies are allowed to
130-          conflict with those specified by the project.
131-
132-          [env: UV_NO_ENV_FILE=]
133-
134-      --with-editable <WITH_EDITABLE>
135-          Run with the given packages installed in editable mode.
136-
137-          When used in a project, these dependencies will be layered on top of the project
138:          environment in a separate, ephemeral environment. These dependencies are allowed to
139-          conflict with those specified by the project.
140-
141-      --with-requirements <WITH_REQUIREMENTS>
142-          Run with the packages listed in the given files.
143-
144-          The following formats are supported: `requirements.txt`, `.py` files with inline metadata,
145-          and `pylock.toml`.
146-
147:          The same environment semantics as `--with` apply.
148-
149-          Using `pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, or `setup.cfg` files is not allowed.
150-
151-      --isolated
152:          Run the command in an isolated virtual environment.
153-
154:          Usually, the project environment is reused for performance. This option forces a fresh
155:          environment to be used for the project, enforcing strict isolation between dependencies
156-          and declaration of requirements.
157-
158-          An editable installation is still used for the project.
159-
160-          When used with `--with` or `--with-requirements`, the additional dependencies will still
161:          be layered in a second environment.
162-
163-          [env: UV_ISOLATED=]
164-
165-      --active
166:          Prefer the active virtual environment over the project's virtual environment.
167-
168:          If the project virtual environment is active or no virtual environment is active, this has
169-          no effect.
170-
171-      --no-sync
172:          Avoid syncing the virtual environment.
173-
174-          Implies `--frozen`, as the project dependencies will be ignored (i.e., the lockfile will
175:          not be updated, since the environment will not be synced regardless).
176-
177-          [env: UV_NO_SYNC=]
178-
179-      --locked
180-          Assert that the `uv.lock` will remain unchanged.
--
190-          Instead of checking if the lockfile is up-to-date, uses the versions in the lockfile as
191-          the source of truth. If the lockfile is missing, uv will exit with an error. If the
192-          `pyproject.toml` includes changes to dependencies that have not been included in the
193:          lockfile yet, they will not be present in the environment.
194-

--

208-
209-      --all-packages

213-
